are reproduced here, followed by the NOTICE files the crates ship, then by the code and data
from other projects in RustGIS's own crates. Generated from Cargo.lock and
xtask/src/notices.rs by `cargo xtask notices`.

/// The PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_PUA: [u16; 18] = [
    0xE78D, 0xE78E, 0xE78F, 0xE790, 0xE791, 0xE792, 0xE793, 0xE794, 0xE795, 0xE796, 0xE81E, 0xE826,
    0xE82B, 0xE82C, 0xE832, 0xE843, 0xE854, 0xE864,
];

/// The bytes corresponding to the PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_BYTES: [[u8; 2]; 18] = [
    [0xA6, 0xD9],
    [0xA6, 0xDA],
    [0xA6, 0xDB],
    [0xA6, 0xDC],
    [0xA6, 0xDD],
    [0xA6, 0xDE],
    [0xA6, 0xDF],
    [0xA6, 0xEC],
    [0xA6, 0xED],
    [0xA6, 0xF3],
    [0xFE, 0x59],
    [0xFE, 0x61],
    [0xFE, 0x66],
    [0xFE, 0x67],
    [0xFE, 0x6D],
    [0xFE, 0x7E],
    [0xFE, 0x90],
    [0xFE, 0xA0],
];

#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include "../../limbs/limbs.h"

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT_AFFINE;


#endif // OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

// SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
// WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
// OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF OR IN
// CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<core::array::TryFromSliceError> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<KeyRejected> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

copyright statement(s).

components as distributed by the Copyright Holder(s).

deleting, or substituting -- in part or in whole -- any of the

the Font Software to a new environment.

writer or other person who contributed to the Font Software.

# Code

ttf-parser
Code from ttf-parser was used/adapted for the following parts:
- Reader, Writer, LazyArray16 and LazyArray16Iter
- `name` table parsing.
- `hmtx` table parsing.
- Most of the `cff` parsing logic as well auxiliary structs and functions
such as `calc_subroutine_bias`, `SIDMetadata`, `CIDMetadata` `RealNumber`,
`Index`, `IndexSize`, `OffsetSize`, `FDSelect`, `ArgumentStack`, `Top Dict` and `FONT Dict`,
`DictionaryParser`.

====================================================================================================
NOTICE of terrio-crs
====================================================================================================

terrio-crs
Licensed under the MIT License (see LICENSE).

This crate adapts algorithms and data from the projects below. The copyright in those algorithms
and data remains with their authors, whose notices follow.

Each source file derived from another project's source files starts with SPDX headers: the
copyright lines of those source files, copied verbatim, the licenses that apply, and the files it
was translated or adapted from. The REUSE.toml of the terrio repository
(https://github.com/opengeos/terrio) records the copyright and license of the data files.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PROJ (https://github.com/OSGeo/PROJ)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Terrio files derived from PROJ source files (each file's header names them):
  crates/terrio-crs/src/datum.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/def.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/grid.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/math.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/params.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/projections.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/projections/perspective.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/projections/polyhedral.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/proj_write.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/rotation.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/route.rs
  crates/terrio-crs/src/wkt2.rs

The two-point form of the Hotine oblique Mercator follows src/projections/omerc.cpp in PROJ 9.8.1.

The table crates/terrio-crs/data/grids.tsv (scripts/gen_grid_catalog.py) is drawn from PROJ
9.9.0's database (data/sql/grid_alternatives.sql): each grid's names, download URL, and whether
the URL gives the file and the grid has an open licence.

The test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/proj_ellipsoid_shape.json is PROJ 9.9.0's output
(pyproj 3.8.0, scripts/gen_proj_ellipsoid_shape.py): the coordinates and ellipsoids PROJ gives
PROJ strings and WKTs, by its ellipsoid and datum definitions (src/ellps.cpp, src/datums.cpp)
and, through its database, EPSG's transformations (EPSG dataset, see below).

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
EPSG Geodetic Parameter Dataset
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

crates/terrio-crs/data/epsg.tsv.zst, datums.tsv.zst, epsg_vertical.tsv.zst and point_motion.tsv,
and the EPSG definitions in crates/terrio-crs/data/esri.tsv.zst, are derived from the EPSG
Geodetic Parameter Dataset (https://epsg.org), owned by the International Association of Oil &
Gas Producers (IOGP), through PROJ's database and GDAL. They are redistributed under the EPSG
Dataset Terms of Use (https://epsg.org/terms-of-use.html), with this attribution. They are not the EPSG Dataset itself, and
terrio is not endorsed by IOGP. The EPSG method and parameter names and codes in
crates/terrio-crs/src/projjson_write.rs and crates/terrio/src/drivers/vector/projjson.rs come
from the same dataset.
crates/terrio-crs/src/projjson_db.tsv.zst is derived from the same dataset (EPSG v12.029), through
PROJ 9.7.1's database, under the same terms; the EPSG method and parameter names and codes in
crates/terrio-crs/src/projjson_gdal.rs come from it too.
The EPSG names, areas of use and projection method names in
crates/terrio-crs/data/crs_catalog.tsv.zst are derived from the same dataset (EPSG v13.102),
through PROJ 9.9.0's database and GDAL (scripts/gen_crs_catalog.py), under the same terms.
The EPSG names and aliases in crates/terrio-crs/data/crs_names.tsv.zst are derived from the
same dataset, through PROJ 9.9.0's database (scripts/gen_crs_names.py), under the same terms.
So are the EPSG WKTs and points of the test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/wkt_unresolved.json
(PROJ 9.9.0's WKTs of EPSG CRSs and its placements of points in them, by
scripts/gen_wkt_unresolved.py).
So are the EPSG WKTs, datums and transformations of the test data in
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/: grads_and_prime_meridians.json, wkt_datum_shift.json,
wkt1_axes.json, wkt1_proj_reads.json, wkt2_bound.json and proj_ellipsoid_shape.json (PROJ 9.9.0's
WKTs and placements of points, by scripts/gen_grads_and_prime_meridians.py,
gen_wkt_datum_shift.py, gen_wkt1_axes.py, gen_wkt1_proj_reads.py, gen_wkt2_bound.py and
gen_proj_ellipsoid_shape.py).
So are the EPSG codes, names and replacements of the deprecated EPSG CRSs in the test data
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/epsg_deprecated.json (PROJ 9.9.0's placements of points in them, by
scripts/gen_epsg_deprecated.py).
The EPSG rows of the test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/proj_string_export.tsv and
wkt1_strict_export.tsv (PROJ 9.9.0's PROJ strings and WKT 1 of EPSG CRSs, by
scripts/gen_proj_string_export.py and scripts/gen_wkt1_strict_export.py) and geokeys_gdal.tsv
(GDAL 3.12.1's GeoKeys and WKT 1 of EPSG CRSs, by scripts/gen_geokeys_gdal.py) are derived from
the same dataset, under the same terms, as are the EPSG rows of crs_accessors.tsv (PROJ 9.9.0's
axis orders, ellipsoids and conversion parameters of EPSG CRSs, by scripts/gen_crs_accessors.py).
The EPSG rows of crates/terrio-crs/data/gdal_towgs84.tsv.zst and of the test data
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/gdal_proj4_export.tsv and gdal_proj4_export_inputs.tsv (GDAL
3.13.3's datum transformations to WGS 84 and geoid models of EPSG CRSs in their PROJ strings, and
GDAL's WKT of them, by scripts/gen_gdal_towgs84.py) are derived
from the same dataset, under the same terms.
The EPSG rows of the test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/crs_list_changes.json (the names,
deprecation, areas of use and bounds of EPSG CRSs added or changed between PROJ 9.8.1's and PROJ 9.9.0's
databases, by scripts/gen_crs_list_changes.py) are derived from the same dataset, under the same
terms.
The test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/helmert_to_wgs84.tsv (PROJ 9.9.0's datum
transformations to WGS 84 of EPSG geographic CRSs, by scripts/gen_helmert_to_wgs84.py) is derived
from the same dataset, under the same terms.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Esri projection engine data
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

crates/terrio-crs/data/esri.tsv.zst, and the Esri datum names in
crates/terrio-crs/data/datums.tsv.zst, are derived, through PROJ 9.9.0's database, from Esri's
projection engine documentation (https://github.com/Esri/projection-engine-db-doc), modified.
The ESRI names, areas of use and projection method names in
crates/terrio-crs/data/crs_catalog.tsv.zst and the ESRI aliases in
crates/terrio-crs/data/crs_names.tsv.zst are derived from the same documentation (ArcGIS Pro
3.6), through PROJ 9.9.0's database. The ESRI WKTs of the test data
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/grads_and_prime_meridians.json, wkt_unresolved.json and
wkt_datum_shift.json are derived from it through PROJ 9.9.0's database, as are the ESRI rows of the test data
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/proj_string_export.tsv and wkt1_strict_export.tsv (PROJ 9.9.0's
PROJ strings and WKT 1 of ESRI CRSs), geokeys_gdal.tsv (GDAL 3.12.1's GeoKeys of ESRI CRSs) and
crs_accessors.tsv (PROJ 9.9.0's axis orders, ellipsoids and conversion parameters of ESRI CRSs).
So are the ESRI rows of crates/terrio-crs/data/gdal_towgs84.tsv.zst and of the test data
crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/gdal_proj4_export.tsv and gdal_proj4_export_inputs.tsv (GDAL 3.13.3's
datum transformations to WGS 84 of ESRI CRSs in their PROJ strings, and their WKT, by
scripts/gen_gdal_towgs84.py).
The ESRI rows of crs_list_changes.json (the names, deprecation, areas of use and bounds of
ESRI CRSs that changed between PROJ 9.8.1's and PROJ 9.9.0's databases) are derived from it
the same way.
The ESRI celestial bodies and their semi-major axes in crates/terrio-crs/src/bodies.rs are derived
from the same documentation, through PROJ 9.9.0's database (scripts/gen_celestial_bodies.py).
The ESRI names in the test data crates/terrio-crs/tests/data/wkt1_proj_reads.json (Makassar /
NEIEZ as an ESRI `.prj` names it, `D_Makassar`) and wkt2_bound.json (`D_OSGB_1936`) follow the
same documentation.

  Copyright © 2010-2025 Esri

Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0, reproduced at the end of this file.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A5, DGGAL and gl-matrix (through PROJ's polyhedral projections)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

crates/terrio-crs/src/projections/polyhedral.rs is translated from PROJ 9.9.0's polyhedral
Snyder equal-area projections (src/projections/polyhedral.cpp and the files it includes, by
Felix Palmer), which are derived from A5 (https://github.com/felixpalmer/a5) and, in
src/projections/polyhedral/snyder.h, from DGGAL (https://github.com/ecere/dggal), and in
src/vec3.h from gl-matrix.

  Copyright (c) A5 contributors

  Copyright (c) 2014-2025, Ecere Corporation

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
IAU_2015 definitions
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

crates/terrio-crs/data/iau.tsv.zst and iau-source.json are derived from PROJ 9.9.0's
data/sql/iau.sql, Git blob 900564ccf2e1f77fb708ddc2c8544843b29bc24a, generated by
scripts/build_db_from_iau.py (Copyright (c) 2021, Hobu Inc; author Even Rouault), under PROJ's
license (see the PROJ section). The source planetary reference is the IAU Working Group report
at https://doi.org/10.1007/s10569-017-9805-5 .
The IAU_2015 names in crates/terrio-crs/data/crs_catalog.tsv.zst, and the IAU_2015 celestial
bodies, their NAIF codes and semi-major axes in crates/terrio-crs/src/bodies.rs, come from the same
source, through PROJ 9.9.0's database.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CRS benchmark reference data
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The pair-4979-4978, pair-mars-iau, form-27700-projjson, seed-tpers, edge-tpers-back,
seed-igh, seed-ob_tran-moll, method-qsc, method-healpix, method-rhealpix and method-omerc-2pt numeric fixtures are from Michael Sumner's rust-proj-capability
(https://github.com/mdsumner/rust-proj-capability), revision
ddd9fac70ec370acd9651ebb8b46035207347ad5, generated with PROJ 9.9.0.
The CRS-study oracle version is retained separately from projection-suite data.
The repository carries no license yet; terrio has asked its author for one (see
LICENSES/LicenseRef-rust-proj-capability.txt in https://github.com/opengeos/terrio).

====================================================================================================
Colour maps. Scientific colour maps sampled at evenly spaced stops (scientific.rs): Viridis, Plasma,
Inferno and Magma by Stéfan van der Walt and Nathaniel Smith (CC0-1.0), and Cividis by Jamie R.
Nuñez, Christopher R. Anderton and Ryan S. Renslow, as matplotlib ships them; Turbo by Anton
Mikhailov, Copyright 2019 Google LLC (Apache-2.0, whose text is in LICENSE); Batlow, Vik, Roma and
Oleron from Fabio Crameri's Scientific colour maps, through cmcrameri (MIT); Balance, Haline, Deep
and Topo from cmocean (MIT). The matplotlib colour map library (matplotlib.rs), from rio-tiler's
256-colour tables (https://github.com/cogeotiff/rio-tiler), each reduced to the fewest stops that
reproduce it: matplotlib's maps (among them the ColorBrewer maps, Apache-2.0, and the gist maps from
Yorick), cmocean's, and rio-tiler's cfastie, rplumbo and schwarzwald.
In rustgis-map: crates/map/src/color/scientific.rs, crates/map/src/color/matplotlib.rs

				AUTHOR

David H. Munro wrote Yorick and Gist.  Berkeley Yacc (byacc) generated
the Yorick parser.  The routines in Math are from LAPACK and FFTPACK;
MathC contains C translations by David H. Munro.  The algorithms for
Yorick's random number generator and several special functions in
Yorick/include were taken from Numerical Recipes by Press, et. al.,
although the Yorick implementations are unrelated to those in
Numerical Recipes.  A small amount of code in Gist was adapted from
the X11R4 release, copyright M.I.T. -- the complete copyright notice
may be found in the (unused) file Gist/host.c.

====================================================================================================
ESRI coordinate system codes terrio-crs's catalog lacks (ESRI:10820 and 65162, in crs.rs): Esri's
projected coordinate systems from projection-engine-db-doc
(https://github.com/Esri/projection-engine-db-doc), as imported into the PROJ database and written
as PROJ definitions by PROJ 9.7.1 (https://proj.org). The notices below are PROJ's licence and the
licensing section of the project's README, at commit aa34139.
In rustgis-data: crates/data/src/raster/crs.rs
====================================================================================================

All source, data files and other contents of the PROJ package are
available under the following terms.  Note that the PROJ 4.3 and earlier
was "public domain" as is common with US government work, but apparently
this is not a well defined legal term in many countries. Frank Warmerdam placed
everything under the following MIT style license because he believed it is
effectively the same as public domain, allowing anyone to use the code as
they wish, including making proprietary derivatives.

Initial PROJ 4.3 public domain code was put as Frank Warmerdam as copyright
holder, but he didn't mean to imply he did the work. Essentially all work was
done by Gerald Evenden.

Copyright information can be found in source files.

====================================================================================================
Coordinate systems by EPSG code terrio-crs's catalog doesn't compute (EPSG:3752 and 22700, in
crs.rs): their definitions are derived from the EPSG Geodetic Parameter Dataset, which is owned by
IOGP (the International Association of Oil & Gas Producers) and published under its terms of use,
https://epsg.org/terms-of-use.html. The definitions were converted to PROJ strings through PostGIS's
spatial_ref_sys: they are not the EPSG Dataset itself, and they only approximate it. The EPSG
Dataset is provided "as is", without warranty of any kind.
In rustgis-data: crates/data/src/raster/crs.rs
====================================================================================================
